It can only move to g3 or f2.
If it moves to g3, `h2xg3` takes it.
If it moves to f2, it's back where it was.
So by playing `d1d2` and allowing `Nf2xh1`, I might actually trap the knight?

If `Nh1g3`, then `h2xg3`.
If `Nh1f2`, then the knight is back at f2.
But if the knight stays at h1, it's not doing much.

Compare this to `h1g1`.
If `h1g1`, then `Nf2xd1`.
Now the knight is on d1.
Moves for Nd1: b2, c3, e3, f2.
If `Nd1xb2`, the pawn at b2 is gone.
If `Nd1c3`, it's active.
If `Nd1e3`, it's active.
If `Nd1f2`, it's back at f2.
So `Nf2xd1` is much more damaging than `Nf2xh1`.

Conclusion: `d1d2` is the best move. It saves the more active rook and traps (or minimizes the impact of) the knight at h1.
